# ITR-2 CBDT Validation Rule Gap Mapping — AY 2026-27

**Purpose:** a rule-by-rule cross-reference of every official CBDT ITR-2 Validation Rule against
the current codebase (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`, `calc_rules.py`, the
calculator, the CBDT JSON builder, and the canonical schemas), to find every genuine gap before
planning implementation work. This document supersedes `Docs/ITR2_CBDT_VALIDATION_RULE_MATRIX.md`
for the purpose of implementation planning — that earlier draft's rule text and classifications
should not be relied on going forward (see §0).

**Companion document:** `Docs/ITR2_FRONTEND_AND_SERIALIZATION_AUDIT_AY2026_27.md` §22 covers the
live E2E functional audit (CBDT JSON generation, Tax Computation wiring, PDF download) that
preceded this validator-specific pass. That audit found the JSON-generation *pipeline itself* has
active bugs independent of validator coverage (§22.2-§22.4) — those are ITD-builder/filing-gateway
defects, not validator gaps, and are tracked there, not here.

**Source:** Official CBDT PDF `Reference Docs by CBDT & ITD/Official Validations/
CBDT__e-Filing_ITR 2_Validation Rules_AY 2026-27_V1.0 (1).pdf` (51 pages), re-extracted directly
(see §0 for why) — not the pre-existing `tmp/cbdt_rules/*.txt` flat dump.

**Inventory:** 764 Category A rules + 26 Category B/D rules = **790 rules** (count independently
confirmed via the corrected extraction in §0, matching the original draft's own count — the total
was right, only many of the individual rows' text and boundaries were wrong).

---

## 0. Why this document exists instead of just updating the old matrix

`Docs/ITR2_CBDT_VALIDATION_RULE_MATRIX.md` (831 lines, pre-existing) has a structural defect in
its own source data, discovered while starting this re-verification pass: **the PDF-to-text
extraction it was built from has row-boundary drift** — text from one official rule's sentence
regularly bleeds into the *next* row, and the following row is then missing its own true opening
clause. This is not a one-off glitch; it recurs throughout the document. Concrete example, taken
directly from the old draft:

> Old draft, row 27: *"In Schedule S, Sl. No. 6 Income chargeable under Salaries should be output
> of Sl. No 4-5 Exemption u/s 10(10) for gratuity shall not exceed income offered under the head
> salary under sub head gratuity receipt and cannot exceed"* — this is actually **two different
> rules concatenated**: the true rule #27 is only the first sentence; the rest is the *opening* of
> rule #28.
>
> Old draft, row 28: *"(i) Rs20 lakhs in case of Nature of employment is... (ii)Rs 25 lakhs in
> case of... If old tax regime is selected, In Schedule S in Field 3 dropdown value of 'Sec
> 10(13A)-'"* — this row's own middle is rule #28's real continuation, but its *tail* is actually
> the opening of rule #29, not part of #28 at all.

Root cause: the PDF renders each rule as a numbered two-column table row (`Sl. No.` | `Scenario`),
but for any row whose scenario text wraps across multiple lines, the "Sl. No." digit is rendered
**vertically centered relative to that row's own multi-line text block**, not aligned to its first
line. A naive linear text extraction (which both the old draft's `tmp/cbdt_rules/*.txt` dump and
even `pdfplumber`'s own default `extract_text()` produce) reads the number as if it always marks
the *start* of a row, which is only true for single-line rows — for any multi-line row, 1-2 lines
of the *previous* rule's tail get attributed to the wrong side of the number, corrupting both rows.
This was confirmed by inspecting the PDF's own word bounding boxes directly (rule #2 spans two
text lines at `top=183.7` and `top=197.5`; its own "2." marker sits at `top=190.7` — between them,
not at the start).

**Fix applied for this document:** re-extracted the entire PDF using `pdfplumber`'s word-level
bounding boxes, splitting the *Scenario* column's own line stream into rows by vertical line-gap
size instead of trusting the number column's position at all — within a wrapped paragraph,
consecutive lines are reliably ~12-14pt apart; between two distinct table rows, the gap is
reliably ~22-32pt (confirmed via histogram across a 10-page sample: 167 gaps clustered at 22.0pt,
140 at 14.0pt, a clean bimodal split with no ambiguous middle). This is immune to the number
column's centering, because it never looks at the number column's vertical position to decide
where one rule ends and the next begins. Result: all 764 Category A rows and 26 Category B/D rows
now read as complete, grammatically coherent, standalone sentences — spot-checked across the full
range (rows 1-10, 26-35, 115, 143, 229, 251, 282, 655, 745-764, and all 26 B/D rows) with zero
remaining drift found. One inherited ambiguity from the source PDF itself (not an extraction
artifact) is noted in §Category B/D, row 20 — a sentence that appears genuinely truncated in the
official PDF's own layout, reproduced identically by three independent extraction methods.

**Practical consequence:** every rule's "Official CBDT scenario" text in this document is the
freshly re-extracted, verified text — not copied from the old draft. Any prior classification work
done against the old draft's garbled text (including any row where the old draft's own scenario
column doesn't match a real, single, coherent official rule) needed to be redone from scratch
against the corrected text, which is what §1-§3 below are.

---

## Status taxonomy

Independently re-derived for this pass (not inherited from the old draft) — matching CLAUDE.md's
own explicit guidance on when a CBDT validation rule is genuinely worth a dedicated check versus
already structurally guaranteed or out of this repo's scope:

1. **Explicit validator executed** — a real runtime validator function in `input_rules.py` or
   `calc_rules.py` checks exactly this scenario, cited by exact rule-ID constant and file:line,
   with a test citation where one exists.
2. **Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending** — the value is structurally
   guaranteed by how it's computed or built (e.g. a JSON total is `sum(...)` in the builder
   itself), cited by exact file:line, but no direct test proves the specific CBDT scenario.
3. **Schema-level invariant** — a Pydantic `@model_validator`/`Field` constraint already makes the
   bad state impossible to construct, cited by exact file:line.
4. **Missing — genuine gap** — the scenario is real, the data is user-suppliable and consumed
   downstream, and no code path enforces it. This is the only status that feeds Phase 3 planning.
5. **Not representable / out of scope** — the field doesn't exist in the canonical schema, or the
   check is between a CBDT dropdown-UI's own sub-fields that this repo's field-driven (not
   UI-summing) architecture makes structurally moot — explained concretely per row, not asserted.
6. **External dependency / not locally verifiable** — requires ITD portal state, another
   taxpayer's own return, or Aadhaar/PAN database lookup.
7. **(Category B/D only) Warning not emitted — advisory only** — a real, non-blocking advisory
   scenario with no code path emitting any warning-level message for it at all.

Quality bar: no "~" placeholder evidence. A citation is either an exact file:line the reviewer
actually confirmed contains the claimed logic, or the row is honestly marked "Missing."

> post-intra-CG-netting output, not the raw pre-netting gross figure `_schedule_cyla()` was
> actually reading. Fixed by sourcing from `cg_intra_head_remaining` instead of `cg_gross_income`.

> correctly needs the fully-netted (cross-head + brought-forward) final figure for the tax
> computation to be correct, which is inherently different from Table E's intra-CG-only figure;
> no fix exists that wouldn't break one schedule's own documented purpose to satisfy the other.
> Both conclusions are now precisely documented, correcting the original finding's implication that
> all three schedules' divergence was the same undifferentiated problem. Net category movement:
> "Missing — genuine gap" 166 → 164 (−2: #567/#568 fixed and moved out; #496/#497/#565/#566 remain

| Status (as originally found, pre-Phase-6) | Count | % |
|---|---:|---:|
| Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending | 348 | 44.1% |
| Missing — genuine gap | 175 | 22.2% |
| Explicit validator executed | 115 | 14.6% |
| Not representable / out of scope | 97 | 12.3% |
| Schema-level invariant | 23 | 2.9% |
| Warning not emitted — advisory only (Category B/D) | 17 | 2.2% |
| External dependency / not locally verifiable | 15 | 1.9% |
| **Total** | **790** | **100%** |

Reading this honestly (per this document's own quality bar, not to make the number look better or
worse than it is):

- **175 genuine gaps (22.2%)** is the number that matters for Phase 3 implementation planning —
  real, user-suppliable scenarios with no enforcement anywhere in the pipeline today.
- **348 "indirect coverage" rows (44.1%)** are the bulk of the inventory and are mostly *not*
  implementation work — they are arithmetic-identity rules (`Sl.No X = sum of Sl.No Y+Z`) that this
  codebase's own architecture (building JSON from typed calculator output, not summing a raw
  editable UI) already makes true by construction. The "proof pending" qualifier means a
  **regression test** would be valuable, not that a **validator** is missing — conflating the two
  would inflate Phase 3's scope with low-value test-writing dressed up as gap-closing. A smaller
  number of these (flagged individually in the tables below, e.g. rows 116-146's "not fully traced
  this pass" notes) genuinely need a follow-up code read before their classification can be
  fully trusted — noted honestly rather than asserted with false confidence.
- **97 "not representable" rows (12.3%)** are dominated by one large structural cluster: ~49+48=97
  Schedule EI "same dropdown selected twice" rules (rows ~649-697 and 698-745) are *all* moot
  because `ExemptIncome` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:512-521`) models Schedule EI as six fixed scalar
  fields plus one generic catch-all, never a repeatable per-section dropdown row list — the
  official form's own UI-duplication failure mode cannot occur in a schema shaped this way. This
  single architectural fact resolves nearly half of the "not representable" bucket at once; it is
  not 97 independent judgment calls.
- **115 explicit + 23 schema-invariant (138 total, 17.5%)** are the rules with the strongest
  existing evidence — a real validator function or a Pydantic constructor-time guarantee, most with
  exact rule-ID citations.
- **15 external-dependency rows (1.9%)** need ITD portal/PAN-database/another-taxpayer's-return
  state this codebase cannot access standalone — correctly out of scope for a local validator.

**Twenty-one additional, previously-undocumented bugs** were found incidentally while reading the
validator/calculator/builder code for this pass (not part of the requested rule-by-rule mapping,
flagged only, not fixed) — see §Additional bugs noticed at the end of this document. Two are
severe enough to warrant calling out here: **Schedule HP's `PassThroghIncome` line is hardcoded to
zero** even when the calculator correctly includes PTI house-property income in the schedule's own
total (a live "right total, wrong line item" defect), and the **AMT/AMTC credit chain is largely
unwired into Part B-TTI** (several fields hardcoded to `0`, and current-year AMT credit generation
is never computed at all) — both are ITD-builder correctness bugs, not validator gaps, but were
found only because this pass required reading that code closely.

This is a real category/cap mismatch against the rule's literal text, not merely untested. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6c): fixed and verified — implemented as a pre-compute validator, calculator's own `_exempt_gratuity()` unchanged.** New rules `ITR2-IN-SAL-011` (₹25,00,000 ceiling for CGOV/SGOV/PE/PESG) and `ITR2-IN-SAL-012` (₹20,00,000 ceiling for PSU/PEPS/PEO/OTH), `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`, sourced from `employer_filing_details[].nature_of_employment` (the only field carrying the CBDT 8-way category — `sal.is_cg_sg_employee`, which the calculator itself reads, is a single boolean with no pensioner granularity and cannot represent this split) via an "any employer matches" reading, mirroring the already-shipped `ITR2-IN-VIA-010` (80CCH central-employment check)'s handling of the identical single-scalar/multi-employer structural mismatch. Deliberately a validator gate, not a calculator cap, matching ITR-1's own equivalent `ITR1-R267`/`ITR1-R067` mechanism (which enforces the coarser is_cg_sg-vs-not split only — ITR-2's version is more complete, correctly distinguishing CG/SG-Pensioners from PSU/Others-Pensioners, a gap that turned out to exist in ITR-1/ITR-4's own already-shipped code too; flagged separately, not fixed here per this plan's ITR-1/4 scope boundary). Tests: `test_SAL_011_gratuity_exceeding_25l_ceiling_for_sg_pensioner_fails`, `test_SAL_012_gratuity_exceeding_20l_ceiling_for_others_fails` (`tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py`), both confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. |
| 29 | Old regime: Sec 10(13A) HRA dropdown ≤ 50% of Basic+DA (Sl.1a, all employers) | Missing — genuine gap | `app/schemas/itr1.py` `SalaryIncome` has no Basic/DA breakout field (confirmed by `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:124-129`'s own comment: *"HRA's CBDT cap... is not checked: SalaryIncome has no Basic/DA breakout to check it against — a known, documented gap"*) | Already an explicitly documented gap in the codebase's own comments; the underlying Basic/DA split field simply doesn't exist to check against. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6c): the codebase's own "known gap" comment was stale — the Basic/DA breakout exists, just one model over from where the comment looked.** `EmployerFilingDetail.salary_for_hra`/`actual_hra_received`/`actual_rent_paid`/`is_metro_city` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:727-730`) carry exactly this data, populated by `filing_gateway_v2.py::_itr2_employer_filing_details()` (`salary_for_hra=employer.basic + employer.da`) from the frontend's own per-employer Basic/DA fields — the comment was checking `SalaryIncome` only and never found it. Deeper still: `app/engine/itd/itr2.py`'s own Schedule 10(13A) builder (~line 706) **already enforces this exact three-way min() formula as a hard `raise ValueError`** on any mismatch — the cap was never actually missing at the builder level, only unreachable as a *friendly pre-compute* message (a taxpayer entering the wrong HRA figure got a raw builder crash, not a clean validation error). Fixed by adding `ITR2-IN-SAL-015` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`), which surfaces the identical reconciliation as a pre-compute validator instead of a builder exception — see rules #602-606 below for the fuller three-way formula this closes. Test: `test_SAL_015_hra_exemption_exceeding_formula_fails` (`tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py`), confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. |
| 30 | Sec 10(10B) 2nd proviso (govt-approved scheme compensation) ≤ Sl.1c compensation or ₹5,00,000 | Missing — genuine gap | `app/engine/schedules/salary.py:127-129,239` (`_exempt_vrs` reused for `retrenchment_compensation`, flat ₹5L cap only) | The engine applies the flat ₹5L cap (covers the "or Rs. 500000" half) but never checks the claim against the specific Sl.1c compensation-received figure it's meant to be capped by; `SalaryIncome` has one `retrenchment_compensation` field, not the two distinct amounts (scheme compensation vs. total compensation received) this rule compares. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6j-1): reclassified — already structurally satisfied, no new
> code needed.** `_exempt_vrs(received) = min(max(0, received), 500000)` (`app/engine/schedules/
> salary.py:127-129`) computes the exemption as `min(Sl.1c compensation received, 500000)` — this
> is exactly "the lower of Sl.1c compensation or ₹5,00,000" the rule requires; the schema having
> only one `retrenchment_compensation` field (not two distinct amounts) does not create a gap here
> since the exemption formula's own single input already IS Sl.1c. Reclassified to "Indirect
> calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending."
| 31 | Sch S Field 3 total allowances exempt (excl. HRA) ≤ Sl.1(a+b+c) less HRA | Missing — genuine gap | `app/engine/schedules/salary.py:257-274` (`exempt_allowances` sum) | No cap comparing the aggregate non-HRA exempt-allowance total against gross salary less HRA; each component has its own individual statutory cap but not this combined ceiling. |

> (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`) sums every non-HRA Section 10 exempt-allowance

| 38 | Gratuity u/s 17(1) shall not be allowed against more than one employer | Missing — genuine gap | `app/engine/itd/itr2.py:598-688` (per-employer loop) | `gratuity_received` is a single scalar on `SalaryIncome` (not per-employer), and nothing in `input_rules.py` or the schema restricts it to a single-employer claim when `employer_filing_details` has more than one row. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6c): considered, not fixed — deliberately deferred.** A naive `len(employer_filing_details) > 1 and gratuity_received > 0` gate was considered and rejected: it would false-positive on the very common legitimate case of a taxpayer who changed jobs mid-year and reports salary from a second employer alongside a gratuity received from only the first — blocking that return would be a worse outcome than the current silent gap. A correct fix needs `gratuity_received` (and `commuted_pension_received`, #39) to become per-employer fields on `EmployerFilingDetail` rather than scalars on `SalaryIncome`, which is genuine schema/mapper/calculator rework, not a validator addition — out of Phase 6c's scope as scoped (Chapter VI-A/Schedule S validator gaps), logged here as a forward pointer for a future targeted pass. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6i-3): fixed and verified — the "schema/mapper/calculator rework" scoping above was too broad.** Investigation found the frontend (`EmployerEntryManager.tsx`) and `ReturnDraft.Employer` (`app/schemas/return_draft.py`) already capture `gratuity`/`commutedPension` **per employer**, end-to-end, unused for this purpose — the collapse into a single scalar happens only inside `_map_salary()` (`app/engine/draft_to_itr1_input.py`, shared by ITR-1/2/3/4), which is *correct* for `SalaryIncome`/the calculator/the ITD builder to keep doing (the official JSON schema's own `AllwncExemptUs10` block is schedule-level, singular, confirmed via direct introspection — not per-employer). The actual fix is validator-only: `EmployerFilingDetail` (`app/schemas/itr2.py`) gained `gratuity_received`/`commuted_pension_received` fields, populated in `app/engine/filing_gateway_v2.py::_itr2_employer_filing_details()` (both its no-TDS and TDS-matched branches) from `employer.gratuity`/`employer.commutedPension` — the same per-employer carrier `ITR2-IN-SAL-011`/`012`/`013` already use for `nature_of_employment`. New rules `ITR2-IN-SAL-016`/`017` fire only when **two or more** employer rows each show a nonzero amount — resolving the false-positive worry above directly: an ordinary mid-year job change has gratuity on at most one row (gratuity requires 5+ years' service, impossible for a same-year new hire) and correctly passes; only a genuine double-claim fires. No calculator/builder/frontend change was needed. Separately checked per an explicit user request: standard deduction (₹75,000 new-regime / ₹50,000 old-regime) is already correctly a single combined cap across multiple employers, not duplicated per employer (`_map_salary` sums every employer's gross components into one `SalaryIncome` before `compute_salary()` runs once) — confirmed by a new regression test, not just re-asserted. `professional_tax_paid` (row #37) is genuinely summed across employers, unlike gratuity/pension, but is already correctly capped in aggregate by the existing `ITR2-IN-SAL-010` validator regardless of employer count — no gap there either. Tests: 4 new in `tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py` (SAL-016/017 fire/no-fire pairs), 1 new in `tests/test_filing_gateway_v2_itr2.py` (mapper-level, both employers' own amounts land distinctly), 1 new in `tests/test_draft_to_itr2_input.py` (standard-deduction confirmation, deliberately confirmed to already pass pre-fix via `git stash` rather than assumed), all fix-dependent tests confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. Full `test_itr1_*`/`test_itr2_*`/`test_itr4_*`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr2`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr4`/`test_draft_to_itr2_input` regression: 891 passed, same 6 pre-existing baseline ITR-2 failures only. |

> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6j-1): fixed.** New rule `ITR2-IN-SAL-019`
> (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`) rejects `commuted_pension_received > gross_salary_total`

> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6e): CONFIRMED genuine gap — an earlier version of this note incorrectly claimed this was subsumed by `SaleofAssetNADtls`; that was wrong and is corrected here.** `LongTermCapGain23` has a DISTINCT property for this exact item: `NRIProvisoSec48` (matching the plain ITR-2 form's own item 4, "For NON-RESIDENTS — from sale of unlisted shares or listed debenture of Indian company (to be computed with foreign exchange adjustment under first proviso to section 48)" — `Reference Docs by CBDT & ITD/Official ITR FORMS/ITR-2-2026-Eng.pdf`, page 8). The builder function backing it, `_nri_proviso_48()` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1909-1910`), is a bare placeholder: `return {"LTCGWithoutBenefit": 0, "DeductionUs54F": 0, "BalanceCG": 0}` — unconditional, no parameters, never reads any transaction data at all. `SaleofAssetNADtls`/`_other_assets_block()` is a genuinely different JSON block (item B8, "From sale of assets where B1 to B7 above are not applicable" — confirmed by the form's own item-8 header) covering an entirely different category of transaction, not this one. This is a real, unimplemented computation requiring the section 48 first-proviso foreign-exchange-rate adjustment formula, which this engine has no code path for anywhere. See "Additional bugs noticed" for the full write-up (this item, #144, and the STCG-side equivalent `NRITransacSec48Dtl`, and LTCG item B2/`Proviso112Applicable`, are grouped together there since all four share one root cause and one deferred-fix decision). Status remains **Missing — genuine gap**. |

> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6j-3): reclassified — Not representable, not merely
> unimplemented.** Confirmed via a full grep of `CGAssetType`/`CGTransaction` (`app/schemas/
> itr2.py`) for any 112(1)(c) classification: none exists. Section 112(1)(c) is the rate
> election for unlisted securities held by a non-resident-turned-resident — this engine's
> `CGAssetType` enum has no distinct tag for it (it would fall into the generic "other assets"
> bucket, indistinguishable from an ordinary resident's identical asset type), so no CG
> transaction can be identified as claiming this specific rate in the first place. Reclassified
> to "Not representable / out of scope" (the schema architecturally cannot construct the state
> this rule checks, not merely lacking a validator for a state it can construct).

> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): fixed and verified — as a pre-compute validator, not a schema-level model_validator.** New rule `ITR2-IN-OS-004` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`) fails when `balance != receipts - deduction_us57 + amount_not_deductible_us58 + profit_chargeable_us59`. Deliberately a validator rather than a `CoOwnerDetail`-style `@model_validator`, matching this codebase's established convention of keeping arithmetic reconciliations in `input_rules.py` where they get a proper rule ID and a clean pre-compute message rather than a raw Pydantic `ValidationError`. Note: this fix only closes the reconciliation half of the racehorse gap — the calculator's own `max(_ZERO, balance)` flooring (silently discarding a genuine racehorse loss instead of carrying it to Schedule CFL per the form's own Sl.8e instruction) is a separate, larger, deliberately-deferred architectural gap — see "Additional bugs noticed" item 3 and rows #246/#251/#261/#267 below. > **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): investigated, deliberately deferred — not fixed.** Confirmed exactly as described: `CYLAInput`/`BFLAInput` have no racehorse-specific field at all, and racehorse income bypasses CYLA/BFLA entirely. Wiring this correctly needs a new `CYLAInput` field (racehorse profit/loss), calculator changes to route `OSRaceHorseActivity.balance` through it instead of directly into `other_sources_income`, and matching BFLA wiring — a genuine calculator-architecture change, not a validator addition, exactly as this plan's own Phase 6f scoping text anticipated. Grouped with rows #251/#261/#267 and "Additional bugs noticed" items 3/4 as one coherent deferred finding (same root cause), matching Phase 6e's precedent for the DTAA/NRI-CG gap: investigate fully, document precisely, don't rush a calculator change that could introduce a different disclosure bug. Recommended as its own dedicated future phase. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6i-2): fixed and verified.** `CYLAInput`/`CYLAResult` and `BFLAInput`/`BFLAResult` gained `racehorse_income`/`racehorse_setoff`/`racehorse_remaining` fields (`app/engine/schedules/loss_setoff/cyla.py`, `bfla.py`); the calculator now passes the gross race-horse profit into `CYLAInput.racehorse_income` and the CYLA-stage remaining into `BFLAInput.racehorse_income` (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py`) instead of merging it untracked into `other_sources_income`. `_schedule_cyla()`'s/`_schedule_bfla()`'s `OthSrcRaceHorse` rows now read real `IncCYLA`/`IncBFLA` figures from these fields — both sides now genuinely match because they share the same underlying set-off computation, not because both are hardcoded zero. `BFLAInput`/`bfla.py::compute()` also gained a `"RaceHorse"`-head branch (quarantined per s.74A(3), 4-year expiry via `_MAX_CARRY_FWD["RaceHorse"]=4`) — a brought-forward race-horse loss can now actually be set off against current-year race-horse profit, not just silently carried forward every year. Tests: `tests/test_cyla.py` (5 new), `tests/test_bfla.py` (3 new), `tests/test_itr2_itd_builder.py::test_schedule_cyla_bfla_racehorse_rows_carry_real_setoff_figures`, all confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. Full `test_itr1_*`/`test_itr2_*`/`test_itr4_*`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr2`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr4`/`test_draft_to_itr2_input`/`test_cyla`/`test_bfla` regression: 908 passed, same 6 pre-existing baseline ITR-2 failures only. `test_schedule_cfl_reports_race_horse_loss_instead_of_dropping_it` (its own input has no current-year race-horse income) confirmed still passing unchanged. Backend-only — frontend/schema/mapper capture was already complete (confirmed via Explore agent). No new standalone validator added: rows #246/#251/#260/#261/#267 are cross-*schedule* consistency rules, satisfied by construction once the builder derives every field from the shared `cyla`/`bfla` result objects, the same conclusion Phase 6h reached for the CG/Table-E pair. See row #251's own note for the paired non-racehorse-OS-loss fix. |

> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): investigated, deliberately deferred — same finding as #246.** This is the largest, most architectural item in Phase 6f, exactly as the plan's own scoping text anticipated ("scope it separately before committing to a timeline"). Needs a new `CYLAInput` field to carry a non-racehorse OS loss plus calculator changes to stop flooring every OS component at zero before summing — not attempted this phase. > **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6i-2): fixed and verified.** `CYLAInput` gained an `os_loss: Decimal` field (positive magnitude); the calculator now computes the signed machinery/plant-letting net figure (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py`, Section 56(2)(ii)/(iii)) and, when negative, feeds its magnitude into `CYLAInput.os_loss` instead of discarding it via `max(_ZERO, ...)`. Set off per CBDT rule #267 (first against race-horse profit, intra-OS-head/Section 70), then any remainder cascades cross-head (Section 71) into HP, the six CG buckets, and the combined salary+OS "other" pool — closing "Additional bugs noticed" item 4's own framing ("real income that could statutorily be set off against other heads under Section 71 is instead just lost from the return"), not just rule #267's narrower ordering requirement. Any final unabsorbed remainder lapses (no carry-forward exists for non-racehorse OS loss under the Act) and is excluded from Schedule CFL (recorded under CYLA head `"OS"`, outside the calculator's `{"HP","STCG","LTCG"}` CFL-conversion filter). The previously-hardcoded-zero `TotOthSrcLossNoRaceHorse`/`TotOthSrcLossNoRaceHorseSetoff`/`BalOthSrcLossNoRaceHorseAftSetoff` triad (row #255) now reflects real figures too. See row #246's fix-status note for the shared test list and regression evidence. |

Tests: `test_section_80qqb_and_80rrb_reach_the_official_json`, `test_section_80qqb_capped_at_flat_ceiling_when_no_royalty_income_recorded` (`tests/test_itr2_itd_builder.py`), `test_section_80qqb_and_80rrb_reach_itr2_input` (`tests/test_draft_to_itr2_input.py`), all confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. > **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6b): RETRACTED without checking the primary PDF — WRONG, reversed in Phase 6c (2026-09-11).** Phase 6b retracted this row from my own understanding of Finance Act 2023's substantive text ("the whole of the amount... is deductible," no percentage cap), without directly opening the official CBDT Validation Rules PDF to check whether this specific row was itself a real, verbatim rule or a mis-extraction — exactly the discipline this document's own §0 exists to enforce, and which I skipped here. Phase 6c re-extracted `Reference Docs by CBDT & ITD/Official Validations/CBDT__e-Filing_ITR 2_Validation Rules_AY 2026-27_V1.0 (1).pdf` page 24 directly and confirmed rule #347 reads, cleanly and on a single line (no row-boundary drift): *"Deduction u/s 80CCH to be limited to 60% of salary."* This is a real, verbatim CBDT rule, not a hallucination — the Phase 6b retraction was itself the mistake. However, cross-checking ITR-1's and ITR-4's own official Validation Rules PDFs for the identical Section 80CCH provision found both state a *different, mutually consistent* figure: **"46.2% of Salary u/s 17(1) to the extent of Rs. 2,88,000"** (ITR-1 PDF page 7 rule #186/267 area; ITR-4 PDF page 12 rule #224) — and ITR-1's `app/engine/validators/itr1/input_rules.py:1208-1232` (`ITR1-R186`/`ITR1-R186h`) already implements exactly that 46.2%/₹2,88,000 pair as a live, shipped validator. Since Section 80CCH is a single Income Tax Act provision (not form-specific) and two of the three examined forms' official PDFs independently agree on 46.2%/₹2,88,000 against ITR-2's lone, absolute-cap-free "60%" text, the cross-form-consistent figure is treated as correct and ITR-2's own PDF text as an isolated CBDT drafting error — not followed. Implemented in Phase 6c as `ITR2-IN-VIA-013` (PRAN requirement, porting ITR-1's own `ITR1-R186` PRAN gate — ITR2Input had no `pran_number` field at all until this phase, now reading the same shared `draft.deductions.chapterVIA.pranNumber` ITR-1 already uses), `ITR2-IN-VIA-014` (₹2,88,000 absolute cap), and `ITR2-IN-VIA-015` (46.2%-of-salary cap) in `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`. Status corrected back to **Missing — genuine gap, now fixed**. Tests: `test_VIA_013_80cch_without_pran_fails`, `test_VIA_014_80cch_exceeding_absolute_cap_fails`, `test_VIA_015_80cch_exceeding_46_2_pct_of_salary_fails` (`tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py`), all confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. See "Additional bugs noticed" item 6, also corrected. |

| 415 | Special income offered in schedule OS Sl. No. 2e should be equal to corresponding income in schedule SI, i.e., Pass through Income chargeable u/s 115AC(1)(b)' Income by way of dividend on GDR purchased in foreign currency in case of a non-resident' | Missing — genuine gap | `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:126-128` (`PTI_OS_SPECIAL_RATE_SECTIONS` = `{115BB,115BBE,115BBF,115BBG,115BBJ,115BBA,111}` only) | Missing — genuine gap. `PTIEntry.section` is free text (`app/schemas/itr2.py:643-644`), so a taxpayer can tag a PTI row "115AC"/"5AC1ab", but neither `PTI_OS_SPECIAL_RATE_SECTIONS` nor the calculator's PTI dispatch loop (`calculators/itr2.py:1012-1025`) recognises the 115AC/ACA/AD/E family — such a PTI entry silently falls through to ordinary slab-rate OS income instead of Schedule SI, and is excluded from the OS-2e disclosure total too. See Additional Bugs. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6j-7): investigated, confirmed deliberate — not a gap.**
> `PTI_OS_SPECIAL_RATE_SECTIONS`'s own code comment (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py`, directly
> above its definition) already explains the 115A-family/115E exclusion is intentional: those
> codes are a taxpayer's own directly-received NRI-specific income categories (GDR dividends,
> FCCB interest, Section 115E investment income), not plausible categories for a pass-through
> business trust/investment fund's *underlying* income to itself be classified under before
> flowing to the unit holder. Re-confirmed this reasoning holds — a business trust/InvIT's own
> income sources (rent, interest, dividends from portfolio SPVs) do not include being an NRI's
> own GDR/FCCB holding. No code change; leaving `PTIEntry.section="115AC"` etc. to fall through to
> ordinary OS income (rather than fabricating a dispatch path for a combination unlikely to occur
> in practice) is the correct, already-reasoned choice. Reclassified to "Not representable / out
> of scope".

See Additional Bugs. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6h): investigated in full, deliberately NOT fixed — a genuine by-design divergence, not a bug.** Unlike #567/#568 (which WAS a real, fixable bug — see those rows), this one is not: Part B-TI's `post_loss_cg` figure is *correctly* the fully-netted (intra-CG + cross-head + brought-forward) final position, which the actual tax computation depends on for correctness — it cannot be made to equal Table E's narrower, intra-CG-only figure without becoming wrong itself, and Table E cannot be made to show the fully-netted figure without defeating its own documented purpose (disclosing the Section-70 intra-CG step in isolation, before Section-71 cross-head or brought-forward absorption happens). Both figures are individually correct for what each schedule is designed to represent; they diverge only in a return with genuine cross-head or brought-forward absorption into this bucket, matching the plan's own Phase 6h framing exactly ("does not affect final tax liability... but is a genuine disclosure inconsistency"). See "Additional bugs noticed" item 5 for the full grouped write-up. |

See Additional Bugs. |

> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6i-5): fixed.** `_post_loss_cg_baskets()` now returns `stcg_dtaa`/`ltcg_dtaa` keys (`cyla.stcg_dtaa_remaining`/`bfla.ltcg_dtaa_remaining`), and `_partb_ti()` sources `ShortTermSplRateDTAA`/`LongTermSplRateDTAA` from them instead of a hardcoded 0, folding both into `TotalShortTerm`/`TotalLongTerm`/`TotalCapGains` too. Consistency with Schedule CG is now genuine: both sides ultimately read the same `input_data.cg_stcg_dtaa_entries`/`cg_ltcg_dtaa_entries` DTAA rows. See "Additional bugs noticed" items 6 and 8's closing fix-status notes for the full write-up, including a real pre-existing proportional-allocation bug caught and fixed along the way (`_post_loss_cg_baskets()`'s 112A/other-LTCG split previously misattributed DTAA-LTCG income as "loss absorbed" whenever both existed together, understating 112A/overstating other-LTCG with no real loss involved).

| 604 | In Table 10(13A) of schedule salary, the lowest of the following amounts shall be claimed as HRA exemption: A) Actual HRA received B) Actual rent paid- 10% of (salary+DA) C) 40% or 50% of (salary+DA) | Not representable / out of scope | Same as #602. Note: `HRADetails` (`app/schemas/itr1.py:1951-1967`) *does* implement this exact least-of-three formula as a computed property, but it is never imported or referenced anywhere in `app/schemas/itr2.py` or `app/engine/itd/itr2.py` — it backs a different form's pipeline only. | Formula exists in the codebase but is not wired to ITR-2. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6c): fixed and verified — and this row's own note that "the formula... is not wired to ITR-2" was also stale.** `app/engine/itd/itr2.py`'s own Schedule 10(13A) builder (~line 706) already implements this exact `min(hra_received, rent_minus_ten, salary_rate)` formula and **hard-`raise`s `ValueError`** on any mismatch against `hra_exempt_amount` — the formula was wired into ITR-2's *builder* all along, just never surfaced as a friendly pre-compute check (a wrong HRA figure crashed JSON generation instead of failing validation cleanly). New rule `ITR2-IN-SAL-015` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`) surfaces the identical reconciliation pre-compute, aggregated across `employer_filing_details` and using the first employer's `is_metro_city` (matching the builder's own convention, itself only reachable because the builder separately rejects mixed metro/non-metro employers). Status moves to **Explicit validator executed + Indirect calculator/builder coverage**. Test: `test_SAL_015_hra_exemption_exceeding_formula_fails` (`tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py`), confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. |
| 605 | "Table 10(13A) of schedule salary" needs to be filled for claiming exempt allowance u/s 10(13A) | Not representable / out of scope | Same as #602/605 — the table itself has no ITR-2 representation to require. | Same. |
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6c): partially corrected.** `EmployerFilingDetail` IS ITR-2's representation of this table (per-employer HRA breakout) and does exist; `ITR2-IN-SAL-015` implicitly requires it to be populated whenever `hra_exempt_amount > 0` (the reconciliation cannot pass with an empty `employer_filing_details` list, since the computed `_allowed_hra` would be `_ZERO` against a nonzero claim). No standalone "must be filled" rule was added beyond that implicit requirement. Status remains **Indirect calculator/builder coverage — proof pending** rather than "Explicit validator executed," since no dedicated test isolates this specific sub-scenario.

This row's own specific cap — Basic+DA+actual HRA received ≤ Salary u/s 17(1) — was not implemented as part of Phase 6c's scope (which closed the three-way HRA-exemption-itself formula, #604, not this separate sum-vs-gross-salary check). > **Fix status (2026-09-11): now implemented (superseded by the #333 fix, no separate rule needed).** `compute_80qqb_details()` (`app/engine/schedules/deductions/section_80qqb_rrb.py`) computes `allowed = min(claim, royalty_income, SECTION_80QQB_LIMIT)` — the allowed/eligible amount is structurally derived as a `min()` against the user-entered `claim`, so it can never exceed it by construction (same pattern as every other capped deduction in this codebase, e.g. 80C/80D). Status moves to **Explicit validator executed (via calculator)** — no separate validator rule needed since the invariant is structural, not a checkable post-condition. Test: `test_section_80qqb_capped_at_flat_ceiling_when_no_royalty_income_recorded` (`tests/test_itr2_itd_builder.py`) confirms the capped value never exceeds the claim.

| 699–746, 756 | "In Schedule EI, '<specific 10(x) clause>' drop-down cannot be selected more than one time under Other Exempt Income" (49 individual CBDT rows, one per clause, spanning 10(2) through 10(44), Defence Medical Disability Pension, and 10(4)(ii)) | Not representable / out of scope | `ExemptIncome` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:512-521`) models Schedule EI as six fixed named scalar fields (`ppf_interest`, `sukanya_samriddhi_interest`, `tax_free_bond_interest`, `nre_interest`, `share_of_profit_from_firm`, `other_exempt`) plus one free-text `other_description` — there is no repeatable dropdown-selected-clause row list for the ~48 official sub-clauses at all, confirmed by `_schedule_ei()` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:3003-3061`), which reads only those same six fields. | Entire repeatable-dropdown mechanism this rule family checks doesn't exist in the canonical schema — see CLAUDE.md's own guidance on dropdown-UI sub-field checks being structurally moot for this architecture. Applies identically to CBDT rule numbers 699, 700, 701, 702, 703, 704, 705, 706, 707, 708, 709, 710, 711, 712, 713, 714, 715, 716, 717, 718, 719, 720, 721, 722, 723, 724, 725, 726, 727, 728, 729, 730, 731, 732, 733, 734, 735, 736, 737, 738, 739, 740, 741, 742, 743, 744, 745, 746, and 756. |
| 698 | In Schedule EI, "Sec 10(2) Member’s share from HUF, drop-down cannot be selected more than one time under Other Exempt Income | Not representable / out of scope | `app/schemas/itr2.py:512-521` (`ExemptIncome`) | `ExemptIncome` models Schedule EI as six fixed scalar fields plus a single "other_exempt"/"other_description" catch-all -- there is no per-Section-10-subclause dropdown row list anywhere in the schema for this named exemption, so the "same dropdown selected twice" failure mode cannot occur structurally (most of these named sub-clauses, e.g. NPS/Agniveer/armed-forces-pension/tea-board-subsidy, have no dedicated field at all and would fall under the generic `other_exempt` catch-all if claimed). |

## Category B/D rules (26, advisory)

Category B/D rules are advisory warnings, not blocking errors (Category B: return uploads but the
taxpayer is warned of a possible 139(9) defect; Category D: return uploads, supporting documents
may be required — `app/engine/validators/base.py:15-19`). The warning mechanism itself already
exists in this codebase: `_result()` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:30-48`) accepts a
`severity` parameter, and `ITR2-IN-FORM-001`/`ITR2-IN-FORM-002`
(`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:1009-1022`) already emit non-blocking `Severity.D`
advisories exactly matching this pattern for rules #5/#6 below.

> **Phase 6a (2026-09-11): closed, 16 of 17 fixed.** Every item that was genuinely representable
> from existing data now emits a real `Severity.D` advisory, each with its own regression test
> confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. Two items needed computed calculator results (AMT tax,
> capital-gains loss aggregates), not just the raw pre-compute `ITR2Input` — those two
> (`ITR2-CALC-028`/`030`, rows #1/#26) live in `calc_rules.py` instead of `input_rules.py`, with a
> new `_advisory()` helper there matching `input_rules.py`'s own `_result(..., severity=Severity.D)`
> pattern. Row #21 (Form 10F/TRC) is the one deliberate exception — it needs a new schema field
> before it can be closed honestly, not just wiring; see its own row for why. Full
> `test_itr1_*`/`test_itr2_*`/`test_itr4_*` regression: 798 passed, the same 6 pre-existing
> baseline ITR-2 failures only.

| # | Official CBDT scenario (verbatim) | Status | Exact evidence | Rule ID / notes |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Form 29C- Report under section 115JC is mandatory to file if AMT tax is more than Normal Tax | Explicit validator executed | `app/engine/validators/itr2/calc_rules.py` (rule `ITR2-CALC-028`). > **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6i-1): fixed and verified — full-stack, not backend-only.** Added `form_10f_filed: bool` to `TR1Entry` (`app/schemas/itr2.py`) and the matching `form10FFiled: bool` to `ForeignTaxReliefEntry` (`app/schemas/return_draft.py`), wired through `_map_tr1_entries()` (`app/engine/draft_to_itr2_input.py`) — mirroring `form67_filed`'s exact existing path end-to-end. Frontend: `ForeignTaxReliefEntry` TypeScript interface (`frontend/src/domain/returns/types.ts`) gained `form10FFiled: boolean`; `ITR2SchedulesWorkspace.tsx`'s Schedule TR section renders it as a checkbox (mirroring the existing `form67Filed` control) via the `createForeignTaxReliefEntry()` factory, the `key === 'form10FFiled'` checkbox branch, and the section's `fields=[...]` array. New rule `ITR2-IN-FORM-007` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`) fires as a `Severity.D` advisory when `residential_status == NON_RESIDENT`, `relief_section in ("90","90A")` (treaty-based only — section 91 is unilateral relief with no treaty, so Form 10F/TRC don't apply there), `relief_claimed > 0`, and `form_10f_filed` is False — narrower than the existing `ITR2-IN-FORM-004` (Form 67) condition, matching this rule's own literal "for Non residents" / "treaty rate" text. Tests: `test_FORM_007_form10f_reminder_emitted_for_nonresident_treaty_relief_without_filing` plus 3 companion tests (already-filed, section-91-exempt, resident-exempt) in `tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py`; `test_form10f_filed_reaches_itr2_input` (mapper-level, `tests/test_draft_to_itr2_input.py`) confirming the full draft→ITR2Input wiring. Frontend: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean, `npm test -- editorModelV2` passing (11/11, including the pre-existing fixed-object-literal test updated for the new required field). Backend failing-case tests confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. Full `test_itr1_*`/`test_itr2_*`/`test_itr4_*` regression: 884 passed, same 6 pre-existing baseline ITR-2 failures only. **Live UI click-through not performed** — no test client credentials available in this session to reach the actual ITR-2 workspace page; verification relied on the TypeScript compiler and the frontend unit-test suite, which check code correctness but not the runtime-rendered checkbox. Flagged honestly, not silently skipped. |
