## Additional bugs noticed (not requested, flagging only — not fixed as part of this audit)

These were found incidentally while reading `input_rules.py`/`calc_rules.py`/the calculator/the
ITD builder in full for the rule-mapping work above. They are real, concrete defects (not missing
validators) — listed here for a future fix pass, per this audit's explicit read-only scope.


**From catA_chunk1 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:890` — Schedule HP `PassThroghIncome` hardcoded to `0`.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:428-430` correctly folds Schedule-PTI HP-head income into `result.house_property_income` (so the schedule's own grand total, `TotalIncomeChargeableUnHP`, is numerically right), but the schedule's own `PassThroghIncome` disclosure line item — meant to show specifically how much of that total came via pass-through — is unconditionally `0` regardless of any actual PTI HP income. This is exactly the "schema-valid but wrong number" class of bug CLAUDE.md's own audit methodology warns about (see rule #79 above).
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6j-2): fixed — see row #79's own fix-status note above.**

2. **`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:401-441` — eight byte-for-byte duplicate validator blocks.** The check "`tx.full_consideration <= 0 and tx.expenditure_on_transfer > 0`" is appended to `results` **eight separate times** in a row under eight different rule IDs (`ITR2-IN-CG-101` through `ITR2-IN-CG-108`), all with identical messages and identical field paths, for the exact same condition on the exact same transaction. This produces eight duplicate validation failures per offending transaction rather than one, which would visibly spam a taxpayer-facing validation report (8 near-identical error messages for a single mistake) and inflates the apparent rule-coverage count without adding real coverage.

3. **`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:2032-2035` — Table F's applicable-rate and DTAA-rate buckets (`ShortTermUnderAppRate`, `ShortTermUnderDTAARate`, `LongTermUnderDTAARate`) are unconditionally hardcoded to zero**, with no data source anywhere in the builder (acknowledged in the function's own docstring, lines 1950-1959, as a deliberate simplification). Combined with finding #169-173 above, any taxpayer with real STCG/LTCG at applicable or DTAA rates will have a Schedule CG Table F that a strict CBDT validator could legitimately flag as inconsistent with Schedule BFLA's corresponding fields.

**From catA_chunk2 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **Schedule OS `TotDeductions` omits interest expense u/s 57(1).** `app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1104-1111` computes `total_deductions = deduction_57iia + deductions.expenses + deductions.depreciation` but never adds `deductions.interest_expense_us57`, even though that same value is separately placed into the sibling `IntExp57` field of the identical JSON block. Any taxpayer claiming interest expenditure under Section 57(1) (e.g. against dividend or machinery-rental income) will have it silently excluded from the Schedule OS `TotDeductions` total that CBDT rule #191 requires, understating disclosed deductions relative to the individual line items shown.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): fixed and verified.** See row #191's own fix-status note above for the full write-up (the correct figure to add is `interest_expense_eligible_us57`, not `interest_expense_us57` — confirmed against the frontend's own already-correct formula).

2. **`OSRaceHorseActivity.balance` is trusted as raw user input, not recomputed.** Neither `app/schemas/itr2.py:1009-1019` (no `@model_validator`) nor the calculator (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:458-459`) nor the builder (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1259`) ever recompute `balance` from `receipts - deduction_us57 + amount_not_deductible_us58 + profit_chargeable_us59`. Since `max(_ZERO, balance)` is added directly to taxable other-sources income, an inconsistent `balance` value directly changes tax liability without any cross-check.

3. **Racehorse profit never actually flows through Schedule CYLA/BFLA.** `_schedule_cyla`'s and `_schedule_bfla`'s `"OthSrcRaceHorse"` rows are hardcoded to all-zero (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:370,428`) regardless of the taxpayer's real racehorse profit (which is added directly to `other_sources_income` instead, bypassing these schedules, `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:458-459`). This makes several official CYLA/BFLA racehorse-related rows (and the statutory requirement that current-year OS loss be set off against racehorse profit first, CBDT rule #267) structurally unimplementable as written, not just untested.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): investigated, deliberately deferred.** Confirmed as described. Fixed the adjacent, cleanly-scoped half of the racehorse gap this phase — `OSRaceHorseActivity.balance`'s own internal reconciliation (row #194, `ITR2-IN-OS-004`) — but this item (CYLA/BFLA wiring) needs a real `CYLAInput`/`BFLAInput` schema change plus calculator rework, grouped with rows #246/#251/#261/#267 and item 4 below as one deferred finding, recommended as its own dedicated future phase.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6i-2): fixed and verified.** Racehorse profit now flows through `CYLAInput.racehorse_income`/`BFLAInput.racehorse_income` into real `OthSrcRaceHorse` rows on both schedules, and a brought-forward race-horse loss (`"RaceHorse"` head) can now actually be set off against current-year race-horse profit in `bfla.py`. Full write-up, files touched, and test evidence in row #246's own fix-status note above.

4. **No current-year "Other Sources loss (excluding racehorse)" can ever be represented.** Every component the calculator folds into `other_sources_income` is floored at `max(_ZERO, ...)` before being summed (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:433-539`), so a negative OS result (e.g. Section 56(2)(ii) letting-of-machinery loss where deductions exceed rent) is silently discarded rather than being carried into Schedule CYLA's loss-set-off machinery. Real income that could statutorily be set off against other heads under Section 71 is instead just lost from the return; `app/engine/schedules/loss_setoff/cyla.py:46-65`'s `CYLAInput` has no field to carry such a loss at all.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): investigated, deliberately deferred — the largest single item found this phase, exactly as the plan's own scoping text anticipated.** Grouped with item 3 and rows #246/#251/#261/#267 as one deferred finding, all sharing the same root cause (this engine's `CYLAInput` cannot represent any negative Schedule-OS component, racehorse or otherwise). Recommended as its own dedicated future phase, matching Phase 6e's DTAA/NRI-CG precedent — don't rush a calculator-architecture change that could introduce a different disclosure bug.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6i-2): fixed and verified.** `CYLAInput.os_loss` now carries the machinery/plant-letting net loss magnitude (the only current-year OS loss this engine can represent today, since every other OS component remains schema-floored at >=0); it is set off first against race-horse profit (row #267), then cascades cross-head (Section 71) into HP, the six CG buckets, and the combined salary+OS "other" pool — closing this item's own framing that such a loss is real, statutorily set-off-able income being lost from the return, not just an ordering gap. Full write-up in row #246's/#251's own fix-status notes above.

5. **`TaxAccumulatedBalRecPF` totals can silently disagree with their own detail rows.** `TotalIncomeBenefit`/`TotalTaxBenefit` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1044-1045`) are populated from the independent scalars `os_pf_income_benefit`/`os_pf_tax_benefit` rather than summed from the `os_pf_accumulated_entries` detail rows built into the same JSON object one line earlier — the two are never reconciled anywhere, so the filed JSON's own header total and its own line items can disagree.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6f): fixed and verified.** See rows #211/#212's own fix-status notes above (`ITR2-IN-OS-002`/`ITR2-IN-OS-003`) for the full write-up and test references.

6. **`Section80CCH` has no statutory ceiling despite CBDT's own validation catalog specifying one (60% of salary).** `app/engine/schedules/deductions/section_80cch.py` allows the full user-claimed amount with no cap of any kind beyond the aggregate GTI ceiling shared with 80CCD(2) (`app/engine/schedules/deductions/__init__.py:143-150`). If the 60%-of-salary cap is a genuine current-law limit (Agniveer Corpus Fund contributions are statutorily linked to a percentage of "Seva Nidhi" pay), this is a real under-validation that could let an excessive claim reach the filed return.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6b): RETRACTED — WRONG, reversed and fixed in Phase 6c (2026-09-11).** See #347's own corrected note above for the full reasoning: the "60% of salary" figure is a real, verbatim rule in ITR-2's own official Validation Rules PDF (confirmed by direct re-extraction), not a hallucination — the Phase 6b retraction was itself made without checking that primary source. Fixed in Phase 6c using the cross-form-consistent 46.2%-of-salary/₹2,88,000 figure both ITR-1's and ITR-4's official PDFs independently state for the identical Section 80CCH provision (treating ITR-2's lone "60%" text as an isolated CBDT drafting error), plus the PRAN-number requirement ITR-1's own shipped `ITR1-R186` already enforces. New rules `ITR2-IN-VIA-013`/`014`/`015`.

7. **`section80QQB`/`section80RRB` are captured on the frontend draft but never reach `ITR2Input`.** `app/schemas/return_draft.py:1272-1276` defines `section80QQB`/`section80QQBRoyaltyIncome`/`section80RRB` fields, and `app/engine/importers/filed_return_parser.py:645-646` even parses them back out of an already-filed return — but `app/engine/draft_to_itr2_input.py` never reads any of these fields when building `ITR2Input`, and `Chapter6ADeductions` has no corresponding field to receive them. A taxpayer who fills in a Section 80QQB/80RRB (royalty/patent-royalty) claim on the frontend will have it silently vanish before the return is ever computed or filed.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11): fixed and verified.** Full wiring added: `ITR2Input.deduction_80qqb`/`royalty_income_80qqb`/`deduction_80rrb` (`app/schemas/itr2.py`), read from `draft.deductions.chapterVIA.section80QQB`/`section80QQBRoyaltyIncome`/`section80RRB` in `draft_to_itr2_input.py`, computed by new module `app/engine/schedules/deductions/section_80qqb_rrb.py` (flat ₹3,00,000 ceilings, min-of-royalty-income for 80QQB), and mapped to `Section80QQB`/`Section80RRB` in the official JSON via `_VIA_SECTION_TO_FIELD` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py`). A first attempt used the wrong field path (`draft.deductions.section80QQB` instead of the correct nested `draft.deductions.chapterVIA.section80QQB`) — caught by a dedicated mapper-level test rather than a calculator-level test that would have bypassed the mapper entirely. See #333's fix-status note above for full test references. Fixed as part of Phase 6b (`C:\Users\Devansh\.claude\plans\zippy-juggling-sprout.md`).

## Summary of classification counts (rules 191–382, 191 rules)

- Explicit validator executed: 24
- Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending: 92
- Schema-level invariant: 1 (dedicated) + 2 rules that also qualify alongside "Indirect" (#353, #355)
- Missing — genuine gap: 56
- Not representable / out of scope: 18
- External dependency / not locally verifiable: 0

**From catA_chunk3 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **Section 90/90A vs Section 91 foreign-tax relief is conflated.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1146-1149` sums `tr1_entries[*].relief_claimed` into a single `r.relief_90_91` figure regardless of `relief_section`. `app/engine/itd/itr2.py:3968-3969` then reports the *entire* combined figure under `"Section90"` and hardcodes `"Section91": 0`. Any taxpayer with genuine Section 91 (unilateral, non-DTAA) relief will have it misreported as Section 90/90A relief, and `"Section91"` will always read zero. The grand total (`TotTaxRelief`) is still numerically correct, but the required split (rules #526/#527) is wrong. Affects rules 526, 527.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6g): fixed and verified.** See #526's own fix-status note above for the full write-up and test references.

2. **`result.total_tcs` omits spouse/other-person-claimed TCS.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1163` computes `r.total_tcs = sum(entry.tcs_credit_claimed for entry in input_data.tcs_entries)`, never including `entry.tcs_credit_claimed_spouse_or_other`. This feeds Part B-TTI's `"TCS"` figure (`itd/itr2.py:4027`), while Schedule TCS's own declared `"TotalSchTCS"` (`itd/itr2.py:3792-3799`) correctly sums both own-hand and spouse-claimed amounts. The two totals will diverge for any return with spouse-claimed TCS under section 5A. Affects rules 460, 493.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6g): fixed and verified.** See #460's own fix-status note above for the full write-up and test references.

3. **AMT/AMTC credit chain is largely unwired into Part B-TTI.** Several Part B-TTI fields that are supposed to be sourced from Schedule AMT/AMTC are hardcoded to `0` regardless of the actual computed AMT figures: `tax_payable_deemed_total_income = 0` (`itd/itr2.py:3957`, feeds items 1a-1d and `GrossTaxPayable`'s first operand), `"CreditUS115JD": 0` (`itd/itr2.py:3990`), `"TaxPayAfterCreditUs115JD": 0` (`itd/itr2.py:3991`), and `"DeemedIncomeUs115JC": 0` in Part B-TI (`itd/itr2.py:3885`, never wired to Schedule AMT's own `AdjustedUnderSec115JC`). Separately, Schedule AMTC itself (`_schedule_amtc`) only tracks the *brought-forward* AMT credit ledger — it never computes or records the *current year's own newly generated* AMT credit (Sl.3 = Sl.2-Sl.1 per rule #424/#425). Together these make every AMT-dependent Part-B-TTI/Part-B-TI/Schedule-AMTC cross-check in this chunk (rules 422, 423, 424, 425, 511, 517, 518, 519, 522, 540, 544, 545) structurally unsatisfiable for any real AMT-applicable return, though most of them are individually self-documented as known gaps in the code's own comments. Affects rules 422, 423, 424, 425, 511, 517, 518, 519, 522, 540, 544, 545.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6g): investigated in full, deliberately deferred — not fixed.** Matches this plan's own Phase 6g scoping text ("scope and test it as one coherent unit," not a validator-by-validator fix). Confirmed exactly as described, plus one additional finding from tracing the AMT-credit *utilization* side while investigating: `_schedule_amtc()`'s FIFO brought-forward-credit utilization (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:3438`, `remaining_capacity = max(_ZERO, result.amt_tax)`) uses `result.amt_tax` — which is only ever nonzero in a year AMT is currently BINDING (AMT tax > regular tax) — as the "capacity" to consume OLD credit. Per Section 115JD, brought-forward AMT credit is normally utilized in the OPPOSITE scenario: a later year where regular tax exceeds AMT tax, using old credit to reduce regular tax down to (but not below) the AMT floor. This suggests the existing utilization formula may itself need re-derivation, not just the missing current-year-generation piece — a second reason this needs careful, dedicated treatment rather than a quick wire-up. Implementing this correctly needs: (1) the current-year AMT-credit-generation formula (Sl.3 = Sl.2 − Sl.1, i.e. `result.amt_tax` when AMT applies this year, since that already represents the excess AMT adds over regular tax); (2) re-deriving the utilization-year logic against the actual Section 115JD mechanics rather than the current `result.amt_tax`-as-capacity formula; (3) wiring the resulting `tax_payable_deemed_total_income`/`CreditUS115JD`/`TaxPayAfterCreditUs115JD`/`DeemedIncomeUs115JC` fields from that corrected chain. Recommended as its own dedicated future phase — matching CLAUDE.md's own established caution about AMT-adjacent fixes (the digest off-by-one precedent) and this session's own Phase 6e/6f precedent for not rushing a complex, cross-schedule tax-law computation into a validator-sized phase.
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6i-4): fixed and verified — full-stack (calculator, builder, frontend), reading the official ITR-2 form PDF directly (not just code/schema) surfaced four MORE root causes beyond the two already flagged above, per the user's explicit instruction to check the form rather than infer.** All six are now fixed together:
> 1. **New credit generation was never wired.** `AMTResult.amt_credit` (`amt.py`) was already correctly computed but never read; row "xiv" (Current AY, Schedule AMTC's own dedicated slot for this year's newly-generated credit — confirmed by reading the form directly, distinct from the schema's `ScheduleAMTCDtls[]` array which is capped at exactly 13 items, AY2013-14 through AY2025-26, with no 14th slot) is now populated via `CurrYrAmtCreditFwd`.
> 2. **The utilization formula was backwards, precisely as suspected**, and is now `compute_amtc()` (`app/engine/schedules/amt.py`, new function mirroring `bfla.py`'s own `BFLossEntry`/`BFLAResult`/`_ay_start` FIFO-with-expiry pattern): capacity = `max(0, gross_tax_liability - amt_tax)` (Schedule AMTC Sl.3, confirmed against the form's own literal Sl.1/Sl.2/Sl.3 text), computed every year the 115JC comparison is made (not gated on AMT winning) and naturally zero in a year AMT binds.
> 3. **No 15-year carry-forward window was enforced at all** (only schema-shape AY validation existed) — added (`AMTC_MAX_CARRY_FWD_YEARS = 15`, section 115JD(3), Finance Act 2022).
> 4. **Schedule AMT's own `TaxPayableUnderSec115JC` (Sl.4) was surcharge/cess-inclusive**, when the form's Sl.4 is explicitly the raw 18.5% figure alone (surcharge/cess get added separately as Part B-TTI's own items 1b/1c) — a genuinely new, previously-undetected bug found only by reading the form's exact item breakdown; `amt.py` already had the correct raw value on a separate field (`amt_tax_before_surcharge_and_cess`), the builder was just reading the wrong one. Also found and fixed the same conflation in Part B-TTI's own top-level `"Surcharge"`/`"HealthEduCess"` fields, which previously duplicated the NORMAL-provisions surcharge/cess instead of being items 1b/1c (the AMT-side surcharge/cess) — confirmed by the field-name match against the form's own text and their position as siblings of `TaxPayDeemedTotIncUs115JC`/`TotalTaxPayablDeemedTotInc` (1a/1d), distinct from `ComputationOfTaxLiability`'s own `TotalSurcharge`/`EducationCess`.
> 5. **Item 7 ("Gross tax liability") was conflated with item 8 ("Gross tax payable")** — `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py` unconditionally overwrote `gross_tax_liability` with the AMT figure whenever AMT applied. The bottom-line final tax was already correct (via the separate `final_tax`), but item 7's own disclosure — and Schedule AMTC's own Sl.2, which must read the PURE normal-provisions figure every year the 115JC comparison is made — was wrong. Fixed by reordering the calculator's own steps (rebate → surcharge → cess computed BEFORE AMT, not after) so item 7 is final and untouched by the time AMT even runs; a new `gross_tax_payable` field (item 8 = `max(item7, item1d)`) exists on its own now, never a conditional overwrite of item 7.
> 6. **`amt.py`'s own AMT-applicability comparison omitted surcharge on the "regular tax" side** (only cess was added when `regular_tax_includes_cess=False`; surcharge never was), comparing apples to oranges against the AMT side's own surcharge-inclusive total and making AMT wrongly more likely to bind for any taxpayer where surcharge is material. Fixed entirely from the caller side, as a natural consequence of fix 5: `compute_amt()` is now called AFTER item 7 is finalized, passing that already-fully-inclusive figure with `regular_tax_includes_cess=True` — no new surcharge logic needed inside `amt.py` itself. Proven to genuinely matter (not merely cosmetic) with a dedicated test constructing a real scenario where the two calling conventions diverge in their `amt_applicable` outcome (`tests/test_amt.py::test_amt_applicability_comparison_uses_fully_surcharge_inclusive_regular_tax`).
>
> **Frontend**: `ITR2SchedulesWorkspace.tsx`'s AMT section gained a `ListSection` for `creditsBroughtForward` (previously entirely absent — the backend mapper was already complete and correct, but genuinely unreachable via the app UI). No backend/schema changes needed for this half; the draft schema and mapper already fully supported it.
>
> **Deliberately NOT expanded**: item 10's own "8a + 8c − 9" formula now correctly includes real 8c (`TaxDeferredPayableCY`, ESOP tax deferred from earlier years now payable — real, already-computed data that previously sat in disclosure only, never reaching the actual tax arithmetic) but still treats 8b (this year's own newly-deferred eligible-startup ESOP tax) as zero, since `ESOPDeferralInput` has no field for the gross pre-deferral perquisite figure 8b needs — the same, already-documented, separately-scoped gap rows #481/#544 describe; not expanded by this fix. Row #544 (`TaxInc17`/`TaxDeferred17`, 8a/8b disclosed as their own JSON fields) accordingly remains a genuine gap, unlike its siblings.
>
> Tests: 8 new in `tests/test_amt.py` (chapter-applicability flag, the surcharge-inclusion-fix proof, `compute_amtc()` FIFO/cap/expiry), 5 new/rewritten in `tests/test_itr2_itd_builder.py` (new-credit generation without utilization, utilization without new-credit generation, multi-year FIFO utilization against the corrected cap, a dedicated ordinary-return no-op regression given the calculator step-reordering, plus the existing `deduction_claim`/`rejects_out_of_range_assessment_year` tests re-verified unchanged), 3 new in the frontend `ITR2SchedulesWorkspace.test.ts`, all confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash`. Full `test_itr1_*`/`test_itr2_*`/`test_itr4_*`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr2`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr4`/`test_draft_to_itr2_input`/`test_amt` regression: 907 passed, same 6 pre-existing baseline ITR-2 failures only — including the dedicated no-regression test confirming an ordinary return with zero AMT-triggering deductions computes identically before and after the calculator reordering. Frontend: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean, full Vitest suite green (210/210). **One verification gap, disclosed rather than silently skipped**: no live browser click-through of the new AMT-credit UI was done this session — navigation to the local dev server was denied, and even had it succeeded, this session has no test-client credentials to reach the authenticated ITR-2 workspace page (matching the identical, already-disclosed gap from Phase 6i-1).

4. **Schedule ESOP total is never wired into Part B-TTI.** `"GrossTaxPay": {"TaxInc17": 0, "TaxDeferred17": 0, "TaxDeferredPayableCY": 0}` is hardcoded in `_partb_tti()` (`itd/itr2.py:3989`) with no reference at all to `_schedule_esop()`'s real, correctly-computed deferred-tax totals (`total_attributed`/`total_balance_cf`, `itd/itr2.py:3600-3616`). Unlike the AMT gaps above, this one carries no acknowledging code comment. Affects rules 481, 545.

5. **Capital-gains figures reported in Part B-TI / Schedule CYLA use a different netting basis than Schedule CG's own Table E.** Table E (`_cg_loss_setoff_table`, `itd/itr2.py:1407-1516`) reflects only the intra-Schedule-CG (section 70) set-off, sourced from `cyla.cg_intra_head_remaining`/`cg_gross_income`. But Part B-TI's `CapGain` sub-items (`stcg_20`, `stcg_30`, `ltcg_112`, etc., `itd/itr2.py:3816-3824`) are sourced from `post_loss_cg`, which reflects *additional* CYLA cross-head absorption (e.g. HP loss set off against STCG) *and* BFLA brought-forward absorption on top of the intra-CG netting. Similarly, Schedule CYLA's own per-bucket `IncOfCurYrUnderThatHead` (`itd/itr2.py:344-349`) uses the *raw pre-intra-CG-netting* gross income (`cg_gross_income`), not the post-Table-E remaining figure the form expects entering CYLA. The three schedules (Schedule CG/Table E, Schedule CYLA, Part B-TI) can therefore legitimately disagree on the exact same STCG/LTCG bucket whenever any cross-head loss is set off — this does not affect the final tax liability (which is computed independently from `result` fields, not from these JSON sub-items), but it is a genuine cross-schedule disclosure inconsistency against the CBDT rules requiring these figures to reconcile. Affects rules 496, 497, 565, 566, 567, 568 (and adjacent to 489-491, which reconcile internally but not against Table E).
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6h): this finding actually bundled TWO different situations — one a genuine bug (now fixed), the other by design (investigated and deliberately left as-is).**
>
> **Fixed — Schedule CYLA vs. Table E (rules #257-259/#263/#567/#568):** this half genuinely WAS a bug. The official CBDT Validation Rules PDF states, for all six CG buckets, "Schedule CYLA [bucket] should be equal to Sl.No.8[x] of item E of Schedule CG" — and rule #563 confirms Table E's own "Column 8" is the *post-intra-CG-netting remaining* figure (`cg_intra_head_remaining`), not the raw pre-netting gross figure (`cg_gross_income`) `_schedule_cyla()` was reading. Table E's Section-70 intra-CG netting runs first; its own output is what Schedule CYLA's Section-71 cross-head netting is meant to start from, per the form's own pipeline sequencing — this is exactly what the CBDT rule text says, not an inference. Fixed by sourcing `_schedule_cyla()`'s six CG "current year income" figures from `cg_intra_head_remaining` instead of `cg_gross_income`; the "after set-off" figures (already correct) are now read directly from `CYLAResult`'s own final per-basket pools rather than re-derived. Tests: `test_schedule_cg_table_e_reflects_real_intra_head_loss_setoff` (extended) and `test_schedule_cyla_stcg_before_and_after_setoff_both_correct_with_cross_head_loss` (new, confirms the cross-head case is unaffected), both in `tests/test_itr2_itd_builder.py`.
>
> **Not fixed, by design — Part B-TI vs. Table E (rules #496/#497/#565/#566):** this half is a genuine, unavoidable divergence, not a bug. Part B-TI's `post_loss_cg` figure must be the fully-netted (intra-CG + cross-head + brought-forward) final position for the tax computation to be correct — it cannot be made to equal Table E's narrower intra-CG-only figure without becoming wrong. Table E, in turn, cannot show the fully-netted figure without defeating its own documented purpose of disclosing the Section-70 step in isolation before Section-71/brought-forward absorption happens. No CBDT-compliant fix exists that doesn't break one schedule to satisfy the other; the two genuinely diverge only in returns with real cross-head or brought-forward absorption into a CG bucket, and the divergence never reaches the actual tax computation (which is sourced from `post_loss_cg`/BFLA directly, not from either JSON disclosure). Documented deliberately per this plan's own Phase 6h instruction ("fix if a clean fix exists, otherwise document deliberately rather than rushing a fix that could introduce a different disclosure bug") — matching the project's established Schedule-CG §112(1)(a) precedent for exactly this kind of architecturally-inherent tradeoff.

6. **DTAA-rate short-term and long-term capital gains are never surfaced in Part B-TI.** `"ShortTermSplRateDTAA": 0` and `"LongTermSplRateDTAA": 0` are hardcoded in `_partb_tti()`'s sibling `_partb_ti()` (`itd/itr2.py:3856,3861`); `_post_loss_cg_baskets()` (`calculators/itr2.py:320-366`) has no `stcg_dtaa`/output key for a DTAA-rate CG bucket at all, even though the CYLA/BFLA engines internally track `stcg_dtaa`/`ltcg_dtaa` baskets. A taxpayer with genuine DTAA-rate capital gains would have this income silently absent from Part B-TI's disclosure. Affects rules 498, 499.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11, Phase 6e): investigated further, confirmed worse than originally described — deliberately not fixed, deferred.** This isn't only a missing *disclosure* of an already-computed figure. Tracing the actual data flow: `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:670` hardcodes `stcg_dtaa_signed = _ZERO` unconditionally (STCG-DTAA can never be nonzero at all), and `ltcg_dtaa_signed = ltcg_result.income_dtaa` (line 673) reads a field that is *also* always zero in practice — `compute_ltcg()`'s own `ltcg_dtaa: Decimal = _ZERO` parameter (`app/engine/schedules/capital_gains.py:381`) is never passed a non-default value anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by grepping for `ltcg_dtaa=` across the whole engine — zero matches). So CYLA/BFLA's internal `stcg_dtaa_income`/`ltcg_dtaa_income` pools are themselves always empty, not merely unsurfaced downstream — there is no code path anywhere that classifies a `CGTransaction` as DTAA-rate income in the first place. This is architecturally identical to, and should be fixed together with, the newly-found gaps below (Schedule CG's own `NRIProvisoSec48`/`NRISaleofForeignAsset`/`Proviso112Applicable`/`NRITransacSec48Dtl` blocks): all are NRI/DTAA-specific capital-gains categories the schema has fields for but the calculator never populates from real transaction data. Deliberately not fixed in this phase — needs new `CGTransaction` classification fields plus calculator wiring across at least 5 distinct JSON blocks, not a single validator or one-line fix; see the new item below for the full grouped write-up and why this is scoped as its own future phase.
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6i-5): fixed.** `input_data.cg_stcg_dtaa_entries`/`cg_ltcg_dtaa_entries` (new `CGDtaaEntry` rows, official `NRIDTAADtls`) now genuinely feed `stcg_result.income_dtaa`/`ltcg_result.income_dtaa` in `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py`, which in turn feed real, nonzero `CYLAInput.stcg_dtaa_income`/`ltcg_dtaa_income` — CYLA/BFLA's DTAA sub-baskets are no longer structurally always-empty. `_post_loss_cg_baskets()` now returns `stcg_dtaa`/`ltcg_dtaa` post-loss figures (see #498/#499's own fix-status note), and a real Schedule-SI wiring gap found while implementing this (`compute_dtaa_stcg()`/`compute_dtaa_ltcg()` existed in `app/engine/schedules/special_rates.py` but were never called anywhere — DTAA-rate CG income would otherwise be taxed at the wrong flat rate, folded silently into ordinary section 112/generic-STCG, or for STCG specifically at the ordinary slab rate) is now fixed: both functions are called per-entry, proportionally scaled to the post-loss taxable amount when a cross-head/brought-forward loss absorbed part of the basket. See "Additional bugs noticed" item 8's closing fix-status note for the full grouped write-up (this finding and item 8 share one implementation).

7. **Section 87A eligibility may be over-restrictive for RNOR taxpayers.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1118` computes `is_resident = residential_status == ResidentialStatus.RESIDENT`, excluding `NOT_ORDINARILY_RESIDENT` (RNOR) from Section 87A rebate eligibility. Per the code's own adjacent comment at line ~390 ("NOR is a species of resident under section 6 — only a non-resident is excluded"), RNOR is legally still "resident" for most purposes, and the CBDT rule text for #535 itself explicitly lists "Resident or Resident but not Ordinarily Resident Individual" as eligible for the >5L-old-regime restriction (implying RNOR is otherwise eligible for 87A up to that point) — suggesting RNOR individuals may be wrongly denied the rebate entirely by this stricter gate. Not independently confirmed against a live scenario; flagged for further investigation. Adjacent to rules 533, 535.

8. **Four distinct Schedule CG NRI/proviso-112 sub-blocks are unconditional zero/empty placeholders, never built from any real transaction data — found 2026-09-11, Phase 6e.** All four have a real, dedicated JSON structure in the official schema, confirmed a genuinely different, simpler or NRI-specific shape than the generic "other assets" bucket (`SaleOnOtherAssets`/`SaleofAssetNADtls`) that subsumes several *other* sub-items (see rules #136/#144's corrected fix-status notes above — an earlier pass in this same phase initially, wrongly, assumed these four were also subsumed by the generic bucket; direct code tracing disproved that):
   - **B2 / `Proviso112Applicable`** (LTCG item 2, "listed securities/zero-coupon-bonds u/s 112(1) proviso, or GDR u/s 115ACA — resident individuals only," rules #131-133): `app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1669` hardcodes `"Proviso112Applicable": []` unconditionally.
   - **B4 / `NRIProvisoSec48`** (LTCG item 4, NRI unlisted-shares/listed-debentures with first-proviso-to-section-48 foreign-exchange adjustment, rule #136): `_nri_proviso_48()` (`itd/itr2.py:1909-1910`) returns a bare `{"LTCGWithoutBenefit": 0, "DeductionUs54F": 0, "BalanceCG": 0}`, no parameters.
   - **B7 / `NRISaleofForeignAsset`** (LTCG item 7, NRI Chapter-XII-A foreign-exchange-asset gains u/s 115F, rule #144): `_nri_foreign_asset()` (`itd/itr2.py:1913-1914`) returns a bare `{"SaleonSpecAsset": 0, "DednSpecAssetus115": 0, "BalonSpeciAsset": 0}`.
   - **A3 / `NRITransacSec48Dtl`** (STCG equivalent of B4 — no dedicated official rule number found; only referenced as a component of summary rules #160/#164/#573/#574, which stay internally self-consistent at zero and would never flag this): `itd/itr2.py:1648` hardcodes `"NRITransacSec48Dtl": {"NRItaxSTTPaid": 0, "NRItaxSTTNotPaid": 0}`.

   All four require the same missing capability: `CGTransaction`/`ITR2Input` has no field anywhere to classify a transaction into any of these four NRI/proviso-112 categories (confirmed: `CGAssetType`'s members are all ordinary asset-class distinctions — `unlisted_shares`/`listed_security`/`bonds_debentures`/etc. — none map to "proviso-112(1) listed security," "NRI Chapter-XII-A foreign-exchange asset," or similar). B4 additionally needs the section-48-first-proviso foreign-exchange-rate-adjustment formula, which this engine has no computation for anywhere (a genuinely unusual, rarely-triggered calculation requiring historical exchange rates at acquisition and transfer). Combined with finding #6 above (DTAA-rate STCG/LTCG, architecturally the same class of gap — an NRI-specific capital-gains category with schema-level JSON support but no calculator wiring), this is a coherent, real feature gap affecting non-resident filers with foreign-exchange-adjusted or DTAA-rate capital gains specifically — a narrow but non-trivial population. **Deliberately not fixed in this phase**: implementing it correctly needs new schema fields for transaction classification, the section-48 FX-adjustment formula (B4 specifically), and calculator+builder wiring across five distinct JSON blocks (`NRIProvisoSec48`, `NRISaleofForeignAsset`, `Proviso112Applicable`, `NRITransacSec48Dtl`, plus the DTAA-rate STCG/LTCG figures) — matching the scale and discipline this project's own Schedule-CG §112(1)(a) precedent established (investigate fully, document deliberately, don't rush a fix that could introduce a different disclosure bug). Recommended as its own dedicated future phase, not folded into 6e's remaining validator-sized items.
> **Fix status (2026-09-12, Phase 6i-5): B4, B7, and the A8/B11 DTAA tables fixed in full, full-stack (schema → calculator → builder → tests → validator). B2 (`Proviso112Applicable`) and the A3-side non-DTAA generic bucket remain deliberately out of scope, confirmed still genuinely absent.** Investigated directly against the official ITR-2 form PDF (Schedule CG Table A/STCG, pages 5-6; Table B/LTCG, pages 8-10), cross-checked against the ITR-3 form per the user's explicit instruction, confirming the deferred finding's own "needs historical FX rates" assumption was based on an incomplete read: A3/B4/B7 are BARE, off-form-computed rupee amounts on the form itself — no per-transaction cost-basis breakdown is disclosed, so no FX-adjustment computation engine was needed after all. New `CGDtaaEntry` (`app/schemas/itr2.py`, mirroring `OSDtaaEntry`'s shape, `SecITAct` added since the CG-side schema type requires it unlike the OS-side builder) plus 8 new scalar `ITR2Input` fields (`cg_nri_stcg_stt_paid`/`_not_paid`, `cg_nri_ltcg_without_indexation`/`_deduction_54f`, `cg_nri_115f_sale_value`/`_deduction`, `cg_stcg_dtaa_entries`, `cg_ltcg_dtaa_entries`) — the frontend/draft schema already fully captured all of this (`CapitalGainsEntryManager.tsx`'s `stDtaa`/`ltDtaa`/`ltNriProviso48`/`stSection48` sections, `ltForeignAssets` generic rows), so this phase's mapper work (`_map_cg_dtaa_entries`/`_map_cg_nri_proviso_48`, `app/engine/draft_to_itr2_input.py`) was the only new frontend-facing wiring needed, not new UI. `_nri_proviso_48()`/`_nri_foreign_asset()`/the `NRITransacSec48Dtl` block (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py`) now compute real B4c/B7c/A3a/A3b values instead of bare zeros; a new `_cg_dtaa_rows()` helper builds the official `NRIDTAADtls` array for both A8 (STCG) and B11 (LTCG), wired alongside the previously-hardcoded `TotalAmtNotTaxUsDTAA{Stcg,Ltcg}`/`TotalAmtTaxUsDTAA{Stcg,Ltcg}` aggregate scalars.
>
> **Two additional real bugs found and fixed while wiring this in** (neither named in the original deferred-finding text): (1) a **latent double-bucketing bug**, invisible only because `income_dtaa` was always zero before this phase — `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py`'s CYLA-basket mapping simultaneously folded `stcg_result.income_dtaa` into the 30%-rate bucket (`stcg_30_signed`) AND read it again (as always-zero) into its own dedicated `stcg_dtaa_signed`; fixed by removing it from the 30% sum. (2) `compute_dtaa_stcg()`/`compute_dtaa_ltcg()` (`app/engine/schedules/special_rates.py`) already existed with the correct official `DTAASTCG`/`DTAALTCG` Schedule-SI codes but were never called anywhere in the codebase — without wiring them in, DTAA-rate CG income would have been taxed at the wrong rate (silently folded into ordinary section 112 for LTCG, or the ordinary slab rate for STCG, once the DTAA folding above made the income nonzero) rather than its own `applicable_rate`; also found `_schedule_si()`'s own `section_code_map` had no entries for `DTAASTCG`/`DTAALTCG` at all, which would have silently mis-tagged every such row as SecCode `"1"` (accumulated-PF income) had the SI wiring not been added in the same pass. Also fixed a **pre-existing proportional-allocation bug** in `_post_loss_cg_baskets()`: its 112A-vs-other-LTCG loss-split math previously included `ltcg.income_dtaa` in `total_ltcg_before` while comparing against `bfla.ltcg125_remaining` (which never included DTAA, a genuinely separate CYLA/BFLA sub-basket) — understating 112A and overstating other-LTCG whenever both 112A and DTAA-LTCG income existed together in the same return, with no real loss involved. Confirmed a no-op for every prior scenario (DTAA-LTCG was always zero before this phase, so the erroneous term was always zero too).
>
> **Deliberately out of scope, confirmed still genuinely absent**: B2 (`Proviso112Applicable`) has no corresponding scalar/table item on the actual form matching this feature's bare-entry pattern — it needs its own transaction-classification field, a materially different shape from A3/B4/B7's aggregate-scalar design, and was not part of this session's scoped priority order; still hardcoded `[]`. The A3-side (STCG) non-DTAA "generic other" NRI bucket was not separately investigated beyond A3a/A3b (already fixed) — no further gap found or claimed there.
>
> Tests: 6 new in `tests/test_itr2_itd_builder.py` (A3 STT-paid/not-paid split, B4 net-of-54F, B7 net-of-deduction aggregated across multiple rows, a DTAA-not-chargeable exclusion case, a DTAA-special-rate case confirming the correct Schedule-SI rate/code AND the CYLA/Table-E/Part-B-TI disclosure bucket, and a mixed NRI-proviso-48-plus-DTAA case confirming no double-counting), 1 new in `tests/test_draft_to_itr2_input.py` (mapper-level, confirming `stDtaa`/`ltDtaa`/`ltNriProviso48`/`ltForeignAssets`/`stSection48` all reach `ITR2Input`), 4 new in `tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py` (`ITR2-IN-DTAA-003`/`004` fire/no-fire pairs), 1 new in `tests/test_capital_gains_loss_foundation.py` (confirming `compute_stcg()`'s new optional `stcg_dtaa` parameter is a no-op for every existing caller, including every non-ITR-2 form sharing this function). All fix-dependent tests confirmed failing pre-fix via `git stash` (the schema-import-dependent builder/validator suites failed at collection with `ImportError: cannot import name 'CGDtaaEntry'`; the mapper and capital-gains-foundation tests failed with `TypeError`/wrong-value assertions). Full `test_itr1_*`/`test_itr2_*`/`test_itr4_*`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr2`/`test_filing_gateway_v2_itr4`/`test_draft_to_itr2_input`/`test_amt`/`test_cyla`/`test_bfla`/`test_capital_gains_loss_foundation` regression: 956 passed, same 6 pre-existing baseline ITR-2 failures only (HUF/representative-verification tests, unrelated to this phase). **This is the last of the five Phase-6i deferred items — Phase 6i is now complete in full.**

**From catA_chunk4 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **Schedule 80D silently fabricates "Not Provided" insurer/policy data instead of rejecting** — `_policy_insurance_details()` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:2371-2372`) substitutes the literal string `"Not Provided"` for a missing `insurer_name`/`policy_number` when a claim is emitted to the CBDT JSON. Beyond failing CBDT rules #610-614, this risks a *live* ITD rejection or defect notice, since the official JSON would carry a fabricated placeholder string rather than real evidentiary data.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11): fixed and verified.** See #611's fix-status note above for the full write-up and test references.
2. **Schedule 80D per-policy breakup can silently disagree with the claimed aggregate** — `_schedule_80d()` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:2396-2419`) emits `"TotalPayments"` from the flat `self_premium`/`parents_premium` scalar inputs, never from `sum(row.HealthInsAmt for row in ...)`. A taxpayer (or a buggy frontend) could submit `policies` rows that don't sum to the claimed premium and the builder would emit an internally inconsistent Schedule 80D without complaint.
3. **ITR-2 is missing the ₹35,00,000 Section 80EE loan-principal ceiling that ITR-1 and ITR-4 already enforce for the identical field shape** — compare `app/engine/validators/itr1/input_rules.py:2405-2408` and `app/engine/validators/itr4/input_rules.py:2700-2705`/`4043-4048` (both raise/fail when `total_loan_amount > 3,500,000`) against `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`, which has no such check anywhere despite `ITR2Input.loan_details_80ee_list` carrying the same `total_loan_amount` field. This looks like an accidental omission during ITR-2's build-out rather than a deliberate scope decision.
> **Fix status (2026-09-11): fixed and verified.** See #625's fix-status note above (`ITR2-IN-VIA-009`) for the full write-up and test references.
4. **User-entered `indexed_cost` on `CGTransaction` is never cross-checked against the statutory CII formula** (relevant to Category B/D rule #25 below, but noted here as a correctness risk, not just an advisory gap) — `indexed_cost: Decimal` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:356`) is a raw, unclamped user input rather than an engine-computed value, and nothing in `app/engine/validators/itr2/` or `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py` verifies it equals `cost_of_acquisition * CII(year of sale)/CII(year of acquisition)`. A materially wrong indexed cost — whether from taxpayer error or a bad import — would flow straight into the LTCG computation without any internal check.

**From catBD (Category B/D pass):**

See the Category-A result file's bugs section for the four findings from that pass. One
additional note specific to this B/D pass: `TR1Entry.form67_filed` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:570`)
and `OSSection89A`/`os_section_89a` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:933-940,1043`) are both fields that
appear to have been added *specifically* to support the Form-67 (#3) and Form-10EE (#19)
advisories respectively — the data modeling work for these two warnings looks complete, but the
actual `Severity.D` rule emitting them (the same one-line pattern already used for
`ITR2-IN-FORM-001`/`002`) was never added. These two are the lowest-effort, highest-confidence
fixes in this entire B/D list, since the mechanism, the rule-ID convention, and the source field
all already exist — only the `_result(..., Severity.D)` call itself is missing.
